<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<I>Common Stock</I>&nbsp;&#151; The Company&#146;s Restated
Certificate of Incorporation authorizes the issuance of up to
75,000,000&nbsp;shares of common stock, par value $.50&nbsp;per
share, and 32,000,000&nbsp;shares of class&nbsp;B common stock,
par value $.50&nbsp;per share. At December&nbsp;31, 2005 and
2004, 20,182,531 and 18,651,458&nbsp;shares of common stock were
issued and outstanding, and 3,479,920 and 4,620,130&nbsp;shares
of class&nbsp;B common stock were issued and outstanding,
respectively.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The holders of class&nbsp;B common stock are entitled to ten
votes per share and the holders of common stock are entitled to
one vote per share on all matters to be voted on by the
Company&#146;s stockholders generally, including the election of
directors. Holders of common stock have no conversion rights
while holders of class&nbsp;B common stock may convert each
share of class&nbsp;B common stock into one share of common
stock at any time. In addition, shares of class&nbsp;B common
stock automatically convert into the same number of shares of
common
</DIV>

</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<I>Preferred Stock</I>&nbsp;&#151; The Company&#146;s Restated
Certificate of Incorporation authorizes the issuance of up to
10,000,000&nbsp;shares of preferred stock, par value
$1.00&nbsp;per share. At December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004, there
were no shares of preferred stock issued or outstanding.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<I>Registration Rights Agreement</I>&nbsp;&#151; In connection
with the Company&#146;s initial public offering, the Company
entered into a registration rights agreement with stockholders
holding more than 5% of the Company&#146;s common stock prior to
the initial public offering. The registration rights agreement
provides such stockholders with, subject to defined
restrictions, certain demand, shelf and piggyback rights to
require the Company to register the sale of their common stock.
The Company is required to pay all expenses incident to its
performance or compliance with the registration rights agreement
except for underwriting commissions and discounts related to
shares of common stock sold by stockholders. The last day on
which registration rights may be exercised under the agreement
is April&nbsp;26, 2006.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<I>Rights Agreement</I>&nbsp;&#151; During 2002, the
Company&#146;s Board of Directors approved and the Company
entered into a Rights Agreement. Under the terms of the Rights
Agreement, the Company declared a dividend of one Right for each
outstanding share of the Company&#146;s common stock and
class&nbsp;B common stock to holders of record as of
April&nbsp;12, 2002 and authorized the issuance of one Right for
each share of common stock and class&nbsp;B common stock issued
after April&nbsp;12, 2002 until the Rights become exercisable,
all of the Rights are redeemed or exchanged, or April&nbsp;9,
2012, whichever is the earliest.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The Rights will trade with the Company&#146;s common stock and
class&nbsp;B common stock until exercisable. The Rights would be
&#147;triggered&#148; and exercisable ten days following a
public announcement that a person or group has acquired 15% of
the Company&#146;s common stock or voting rights or ten business
days after a person or group begins a tender offer that would
result in ownership of 15% of the Company&#146;s common stock or
voting rights. Once triggered, the Rights would entitle the
holders to purchase from the Company a unit consisting of one
one-hundredth of a share of Series&nbsp;A Junior Participating
Preferred Stock at a purchase price of $100&nbsp;per share or,
upon the occurrence of certain events, either the Company&#146;s
common stock or shares of common stock of an acquiring entity
for a payment equal to half of market value.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The Rights may be redeemed by the Company for $.01&nbsp;per
Right at any time until an acquirer has acquired the level of
ownership that &#147;triggers&#148; the Rights Plan. The Rights
extend for ten years and will expire on April&nbsp;9, 2012.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<I>Employee Stock Purchase Plan</I>&nbsp;&#151; The Hydril
Company Employee Stock Purchase Plan (the &#147;Stock Purchase
Plan&#148;) was terminated effective January&nbsp;1, 2006. The
Stock Purchase Plan was implemented November&nbsp;1, 2000, and
220,000&nbsp;shares of common stock were originally reserved for
this plan. Under the Stock Purchase Plan, employees could
purchase shares of the Company&#146;s common stock at the lower
of 85% of market value at the closing price on the first or last
business day of each six-month period beginning on each
July&nbsp;1 and January&nbsp;1, except that the first offering
period was an eight-month period commencing on November&nbsp;1,
2000 and ending on June&nbsp;30, 2001. Purchases were limited to
10% of the employee&#146;s regular pay. For the years ended
December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004, 8,589 and 12,400&nbsp;shares
respectively, were issued under this plan. In January 2006, a
final 3,250&nbsp;shares were issued for the offering period July
2005 through December 2005.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<I>Other</I>&nbsp;&#151; In March 2006, subsequent to the
balance sheet date, the Company&#146;s Board of Directors
authorized the repurchase of up to $100&nbsp;million of the
Company&#146;s common stock over the next twelve months. The
Company intends to repurchase its shares, beginning in the
second quarter of 2006, in the open market or in privately
negotiated transactions based on, among other things, its
ongoing capital requirements, expected
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
SFAS&nbsp;130 &#147;Reporting Comprehensive Income&#148;
requires minimum pension liability adjustments to be included in
other comprehensive income. At December&nbsp;31, 2005, 2004 and

<TR valign="top">
    <TD><B>10.</B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B>EARNINGS PER SHARE</B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The Company has presented basic and diluted income per share
(&#147;EPS&#148;) on the consolidated statement of operations.
Basic EPS excludes dilution and is computed by dividing income
available to common stockholders by the weighted average number
of common shares outstanding for the period. Dilutive EPS is
based on the weighted average number of shares outstanding
during each period plus the assumed exercise of dilutive stock
options and vesting of restricted stock and restricted stock
units, less the number of treasury shares from the proceeds
using the average market price for the Company&#146;s common
stock for each of the periods presented. When potentially
dilutive securities are anti-dilutive, they are not included in
dilutive EPS.
</DIV>

    <TD><B>11.</B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B>COMMITMENTS AND CONTINGENCIES</B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<I>Leases</I>&nbsp;&#151; The Company&#146;s lease commitments
are principally for operating facilities, vehicles and equipment.
</DIV>

responsible party at a waste disposal site near Houston, Texas.
Based on the number of other potentially responsible parties,
the total estimated site cleanup costs and its estimated share
of such costs, the Company continues to believe this matter will
not have a material adverse effect on the financial position or
results of operations of the Company.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 18pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<I>Other</I>&nbsp;&#151; The Company has agreed to guarantee 50%
of the total outstanding borrowings of our premium connection
Indian joint venture, an unconsolidated entity, up to a maximum
of $3,070,000 by issuing irrevocable standby letters of credit.
As of December&nbsp;31, 2005, the total amount of borrowings of
the joint venture was $2,135,000, of which $1,067,000 was
subject to the Company&#146;s guarantee. The Company does not
expect this guarantee to have a material adverse effect on its
financial condition or results of operations. As of
December&nbsp;31, 2005, the Company&#146;s investment in this
joint venture was approximately $1,000,000 and the joint
venture, which began operations in the fourth quarter of 2005,
recorded an after-tax net loss for the year of less than
$100,000.

<TR valign="top">
    <TD><B>13.</B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B>EQUITY COMPENSATION PLANS</B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
During 2005, stockholders approved the 2005 Incentive Plan (the
&#147;2005 Plan&#148;), which allows for the granting of various
types of awards to officers, directors and key employees of the
Company. The 2005 Plan provides for a maximum of
1,200,000&nbsp;shares of common stock as to which awards may be
granted, of which 550,000 were authorized for awards other than
options or stock appreciation rights. As of December&nbsp;31,
2005, no awards had been made under the 2005 Plan. The
Company&#146;s 2000 Incentive Plan (the &#147;2000 Plan&#148;)
was adopted in 2000 and allows for the granting to officers,
employees, and non-employee directors of stock-based awards
covering a maximum of 1,950,000&nbsp;shares of common stock, of
which 194,979&nbsp;shares remain available for awards at
December&nbsp;31, 2005.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
During 2005, no options for the purchase of common stock were
granted under the 2000 Plan. During 2004, 212,200 options were
granted to officers and key employees for the purchase of common
stock. Of these, 202,800 were granted at an exercise price of
$28.79&nbsp;per share and 9,400 were granted at an exercise
price of $33.39&nbsp;per share. During 2003, 189,140 options
were granted to officers and key employees for the purchase of
common stock. Of these 185,459 were granted at an exercise price
of $27.165 and 3,681 were granted at an exercise price of
$29.8815. Options granted to officers and employees under the
2000 Plan generally have a term of ten years and vest and become
exercisable in cumulative annual installments of one-fifth each
beginning on the first anniversary of the date of grant.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
Under the 2000 Plan, each nonemployee director is automatically
granted nonqualified stock options each year following the
annual meeting of stockholders. During 2005, however, in lieu of
stock options, each of the Company&#146;s non-employee directors
received a grant of deferred share units as discussed below.
During 2004, each of the Company&#146;s non-employee directors
received a grant of non-qualified stock options to purchase
3,000&nbsp;shares of common stock for a total of
24,000&nbsp;shares at an exercise price of $28.79&nbsp;per
share. During 2003, each of the Company&#146;s nonemployee
directors received a grant of non-qualified stock options to
purchase 3,000&nbsp;shares of common stock for a total of
24,000&nbsp;shares at an exercise price of $27.165&nbsp;per
share. Options granted to non-employee directors have a term of
ten years, are fully vested upon the completion of one year of
service as a non-employee director, have an exercise price equal
to the fair market value of the Company&#146;s common stock on
the date of grant, and become exercisable in cumulative annual
installments of one-third each, beginning on the first
anniversary of the date of grant.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The Company&#146;s 1999 Stock Option Plan (the &#147;1999
Plan&#148;) provided for the granting of options for the
purchase of the Company&#146;s class&nbsp;B common stock to
officers and key employees of the Company. Such options vested
over a four-year period and are exercisable for a ten-year
period. An aggregate of 1,050,000&nbsp;shares of class&nbsp;B
common stock was reserved for grants of which a total of
702,000&nbsp;shares were
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
During 2005, the Company granted a total of 85,400 restricted
stock units to officers and key employees. A stock unit
represents the right to receive a share of common stock on the
date the restrictions on the unit lapse. The restrictions on
restricted stock units lapse over a five year period with sixty
percent of the units vesting on the third anniversary of the
date of grant and twenty percent vesting on each of the fourth
and fifth anniversary dates of the grant. In the event a grantee
terminates employment with the Company, any restricted stock
units remaining subject to restrictions are forfeited.
Restricted unit awards result in the recognition of deferred
compensation. Deferred compensation is a contra-equity account
with an offset to additional paid in capital and is amortized to
operating expense over the vesting period of the award.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">67

<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">
<B>HYDRIL COMPANY</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL
STATEMENTS&nbsp;&#151;&nbsp;(Continued)</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 18pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In addition, during 2005 the Company granted a total of 19,000
performance-based restricted stock units to executive officers.
The vesting of these awards is dependent upon whether, and the
extent to which, specified performance objectives relating to
Return on Capital Employed (&#147;ROCE&#148;) are attained for
the three-year period ending December&nbsp;31, 2007. ROCE is the
Company&#146;s average annual after-tax operating income divided
by average capital employed (total assets and short-term debt,
including current maturities of long-term debt minus current
liabilities). None of these restricted stock units will vest
unless the Company achieves a minimum ROCE as specified by the
Board of Directors for the three-year period and the ROCE
achieved is equal to or greater than the average ROCE for a
specified peer group of companies. If these two minimum
thresholds are met, then between twenty percent and one hundred
percent of the units will vest upon completion of the three-year
period, depending on the Company&#146;s ROCE relative to that of
the peer group for that period. The units will vest completely
if the minimum thresholds are met and the Company&#146;s ROCE is
in the top quartile of its peer group for the period.
Performance-based restricted stock units settle automatically in
shares of common stock on a one-for-one basis upon vesting.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
During 2004, the Company granted a total of 81,900 in restricted
stock units and shares of restricted stock to officers and key
employees. During 2005 and 2004, 2,400 and 600 of these awards
were forfeited, respectively.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
During 2003, the Company granted a total of 76,054 in restricted
stock units and shares of restricted stock to officers and key
employees. During 2005, 2004 and 2003, 2,800, 7,900 and 800 of
these awards were forfeited, respectively.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
Included in operating income in 2005, 2004 and 2003 is
$1,633,000, $618,000 and $243,000, respectively for amortization
expense of deferred compensation related to the 2005, 2004 and
2003 grants.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
During 2005 and 2004, each of the Company&#146;s non-employee
directors received a grant of 2,000 and 2,500, deferred share
units, for a total of 14,000 and 20,000&nbsp;units,
respectively. Each deferred share unit represents one
hypothetical share of common stock. The deferred share units
vest and become payable three years from the date of grant if
the director remains a member of the Company&#146;s Board of
Directors at such time, or earlier under specified
circumstances. Upon vesting, the deferred share units are
settled in cash at the fair market value of common stock on a
one-for-one basis.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; color: #000000; background: #ffffff;"></DIV>

    <TD width="95%"></TD>

<TR valign="top">
    <TD><B>14.</B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B>SEGMENT AND RELATED INFORMATION</B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In accordance with SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;131, &#147;Disclosures
About Segments of an Enterprise and Related Information&#148;,
the Company has identified the following reportable segments:
Premium Connection and Pressure Control.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
Hydril is engaged worldwide in engineering, manufacturing and
marketing of premium connection and pressure control products
for oil and gas drilling and production. The Company sells its
products to steel pipe distributors, major and independent,
domestic and international oil and gas companies and drilling
contractors. The Company&#146;s products are used in drilling
environments where extreme pressure, temperature, corrosion and
mechanical stress are encountered, as well as in environmentally
sensitive drilling. These harsh conditions are typical for
deepwater, deep-formation and horizontal or extended reach oil
and gas wells.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The Company&#146;s premium connection segment manufactures
premium connections that are used in drilling environments where
extreme pressure, temperature, corrosion and mechanical stress
are encountered, as well as in environmentally sensitive
drilling. These harsh drilling conditions are typical for
deepwater, deep-formation and horizontal or extended reach
wells. Hydril applies premium threaded connections to tubulars
owned by its customers and purchases pipe in certain
international markets for threading and resale. Hydril
manufactures premium threaded connections and provides services
at facilities located in Bakersfield, California; Westwego,
Louisiana; Houston, Texas; Nisku, Alberta, and Dartmouth, Nova
Scotia, Canada; Maharashtra, India; Batam, Indonesia; Veracruz,
Mexico; Warri, Nigeria and Aberdeen, Scotland.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">68

<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">
<B>HYDRIL COMPANY</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL
STATEMENTS&nbsp;&#151;&nbsp;(Continued)</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 18pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The Company&#146;s pressure control segment manufactures a broad
range of pressure control equipment used in oil and gas drilling
and well completion typically employed in harsh environments.
The Company&#146;s pressure control products are primarily
safety devices that control and contain fluid and gas pressure
during drilling, completion and maintenance in oil and gas
wells. The Company also provides aftermarket replacement parts,
repair and field services for its installed base of pressure
control equipment. Hydril manufactures pressure control products
at two plant locations in Houston, Texas and one plant location
in New Mills, England.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The accounting policies of the segments are the same as those
described in the summary of significant accounting policies. The
Company evaluates segment performance based on operating income
or loss.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;

customer of the Company&#146;s pressure control segment
represented 13% of the Company&#146;s consolidated revenue and
revenue from one customer of the Company&#146;s premium
connection segment represented 11% of the Company&#146;s

<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">

<TR>
    <TD width="5%"></TD>
    <TD width="95%"></TD>
</TR>

<TR valign="top">
    <TD><B>16.</B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B>RECENT ACCOUNTING PRONOUNCEMENTS</B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In May 2005, the Financial Accounting Standards Board
(&#147;FASB&#148;) issued Statement of Financial Accounting
Standards No.&nbsp;154 (&#147;SFAS&nbsp;154&#148;),
&#147;Accounting Changes and Error Corrections-a replacement of
APB Opinion No.&nbsp;20 and FASB statement No.&nbsp;3.&#148;
SFAS&nbsp;154 replaces APB No.&nbsp;20, &#147;Accounting
Changes&#148; and FASB No.&nbsp;3, &#147;Reporting Accounting
Changes in Interim Financial Statements&#148; and provides
guidance on the accounting and reporting of accounting changes
and error corrections. The statement applies to all voluntary
changes in accounting principles as well as all changes required
by an accounting pronouncement in the unusual instance that the
pronouncement does not include specific transition provisions.
SFAS&nbsp;154 is effective for accounting changes and
corrections of errors made in fiscal years beginning after
December&nbsp;15, 2005. The Company believes the implementation
of SFAS&nbsp;154 will not have a material effect on the
Company&#146;s results of operations or financial condition.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In March 2005, the FASB issued FASB Interpretation
(&#147;FIN&#148;) No.&nbsp;47, &#147;Accounting for Conditional
Asset Retirement Obligations&nbsp;&#151; an interpretation of
FASB Statement No.&nbsp;143.&#148; This interpretation defines
the term &#147;conditional asset retirement obligation&#148; as
used in FASB Statement No.&nbsp;143, &#147;Accounting for Asset
Retirement Obligations,&#148; as a legal obligation to perform
an asset retirement activity, in which the timing and
(or)&nbsp;method of settlement are conditional on a future event
that may or may not be within the control of the entity.
FIN&nbsp;No.&nbsp;47 requires that an obligation to perform an
asset retirement activity is unconditional even though
uncertainty exists about the timing and (or)&nbsp;method of
settlement. FIN&nbsp;No.&nbsp;47 also clarifies when an entity
would have sufficient information to reasonably estimate the
fair value of an asset retirement. FIN&nbsp;No.&nbsp;47 is
effective no later than the end of fiscal years ending after
December&nbsp;15, 2005. The adoption of FIN&nbsp;No.&nbsp;47 did
not have a material impact on our results of operations or
financial condition.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In December 2004, the FASB issued Statement of Financial
Accounting Standards No.&nbsp;123&nbsp;&#151; Revised 2004
(&#147;SFAS&nbsp;123(R)&#148;), &#147;Share-Based Payment&#148;.
This is a revision of SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;123, &#147;Accounting
for Stock-Based Compensation&#148;, and supersedes APB
No.&nbsp;25, &#147;Accounting for Stock Issued to
Employees&#148;. Under SFAS&nbsp;123(R), the Company will be
required to measure the cost of employee services received in
exchange for stock based on the grant-date fair value (with
limited exceptions). That cost will be recognized over the
period during which an employee is required to provide service
in exchange for the award (usually the vesting period). The fair
value will be estimated using an option-pricing model. Excess
tax benefits, as defined in SFAS&nbsp;123(R), will be recognized
as an addition to paid-in capital. We must adopt
SFAS&nbsp;123(R), effective
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">71

<!-- PAGEBREAK -->
<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">
<B>HYDRIL COMPANY</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL
STATEMENTS&nbsp;&#151;&nbsp;(Continued)</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
January 1 2006, which is consistent with the standard&#146;s
effective date. The Company is currently in the process of
evaluating the impact of SFAS&nbsp;123(R).
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In December 2004, the FASB issued SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;153,
Exchanges of Nonmonetary Assets&nbsp;&#151; an amendment of APB
Opinion No.&nbsp;29, to address the measurement of exchanges of
nonmonetary assets. SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;153 eliminates the
exception from fair value measurement for nonmonetary exchanges
of similar productive assets and replaces it with a general
exception for exchanges of nonmonetary assets that do not have
commercial substance. A nonmonetary exchange has commercial
substance if the future cash flows of the entity are expected to
change significantly as a result of the exchange.
SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;153 is effective for nonmonetary exchanges
occurring after June&nbsp;30, 2005. We adopted
SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;153 on July&nbsp;1, 2005. The adoption of
SFAS&nbsp;153 did not have a material effect on the
Company&#146;s results of operations or financial condition.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In November 2004, the FASB issued SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;151,
&#147;Inventory Costs&nbsp;&#151; an amendment of ARB 43,
Chapter&nbsp;4&#148; (&#147;SFAS&nbsp;151&#148;). SFAS&nbsp;151
clarifies the accounting for abnormal amounts of idle facility
expense, freight, handling costs and wasted material.
Paragraph&nbsp;5 of Accounting Research Bulletin
(&#147;ARB&#148;)&nbsp;43, Chapter&nbsp;4 &#147;Inventory
Pricing,&#148; previously stated that &#147;... under certain
circumstances, items such as idle facility expense, excessive
spoilage, double freight, and rehandling costs may be so
abnormal as to require treatment as current-period
charges&nbsp;.&nbsp;.&nbsp;.&#148; SFAS&nbsp;151 requires that
those items be recognized as current-period charges regardless
of whether they meet the criterion of &#147;so abnormal.&#148;
In addition, SFAS&nbsp;151 requires that the allocation of fixed
production overhead to the costs of conversion be based on the
normal capacity of the production facilities. SFAS&nbsp;151 is
effective for fiscal years beginning after June&nbsp;15, 2005.
The Company believes the implementation of SFAS&nbsp;151 will
not have a material effect on the Company&#146;s results of
operations or financial condition and will adopt SFAS&nbsp;151
on January&nbsp;1, 2006.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In May 2004, the FASB issued Staff Position
No.&nbsp;<FONT style="white-space: nowrap">106-2,</FONT>
&#147;Accounting and Disclosure Requirements Related to the
Medicare Prescription Drug, Improvement and Modernization Act of
2003&#148;
(&#147;FSP&nbsp;<FONT style="white-space: nowrap">106-2&#148;).</FONT>
FSP&nbsp;<FONT style="white-space: nowrap">106-2</FONT> provides
guidance on accounting for the effects of the Medicare
Prescription Drug, Improvement and Modernization Act of 2003
(the &#147;Act&#148;) for employers that sponsor postretirement
health care plans that provide prescription drug benefits.
FSP&nbsp;<FONT style="white-space: nowrap">106-2</FONT> is
effective for the first interim or annual period beginning after
June&nbsp;15, 2004. The adoption of
FSP&nbsp;<FONT style="white-space: nowrap">106-2</FONT> on
July&nbsp;1, 2004 did not have a material effect on the
Company&#146;s results of operations or financial condition.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In December 2003, the Company adopted SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;132
(Revised 2003), &#147;Employees&#146; Disclosures about Pensions
and Other Post Retirement Benefits.&#148; The statement requires
additional disclosures relating to pensions and other
post-retirement benefits, which we have included in Note&nbsp;6.
</DIV>

</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
Hydril&#146;s Chief Executive Officer and Chief Financial
Officer have evaluated the Company&#146;s disclosure controls
and procedures as of December&nbsp;31, 2005, and they have
concluded that these controls and procedures are effective.
There were no changes in the Company&#146;s internal control
over financial reporting that occurred during the Company&#146;s
last fiscal quarter that have materially affected, or are
reasonably likely to materially affect, the Company&#146;s
internal control over financial reporting. The Company&#146;s
management report on internal control over financial reporting
is included at page&nbsp;43 of this
Form&nbsp;<FONT style="white-space: nowrap">10-K</FONT> and the
attestation report of Deloitte&nbsp;&#38; Touche LLP on
management&#146;s assessment is included at page&nbsp;44 of this
Form&nbsp;<FONT style="white-space: nowrap">10-K.

Hydril&#146;s code of ethics, known as its Code of Business
Conduct and Ethics, applies to all officers and employees of
Hydril, including its principal executive officer, principal
financial officer, principal accounting officer and controller.
The Code of Business Conduct and Ethics satisfies the
requirements for a code of ethics under Item&nbsp;406 of
Regulation&nbsp;<FONT style="white-space: nowrap">S-K</FONT> and
is available on Hydril&#146;s website, http://www.hydril.com. In
addition, Hydril intends to satisfy the disclosure requirements
of Item&nbsp;5.05 of
Form&nbsp;<FONT style="white-space: nowrap">8-K</FONT> regarding
any amendments to, or waiver from, a provision of the Code of
Business Conduct and Ethics that applies to Hydril&#146;s
principal executive officer, principal financial officer,
principal accounting officer or controller and relates to any
element of the definition of code of ethics set forth in
Item&nbsp;406(b) of
Regulation&nbsp;<FONT style="white-space: nowrap">S-K</FONT> by
posting such information on its website.
